## Serenia

Queen Serenia of Mulhorand was the beautiful fey spouse of Pharaoh Sorenhotep I, who reigned during the waning years of the Second Empire of Mulhorand.

History
When Pharaoh, Sorenhotep I, was still a young man, he was convinced that he needed a queen of beauty greater than any other mortal woman of the Realms. The Pharaoh sent messengers to all, even the farthest part of the world, in search of such a wife. One of the royal messengers returned with the story of the fey princess Serenia. Without hesitation, Sorenhotep organized a caravan to the princess's land and placed a Mulhorandi diplomat as its leader with five thousand soldiers, one hundred wizards, forty bards, dozens of wagons made from gold, cages full of animals from the royal zoo, mechanical wonders from the Mulhorandi workshops, truly a delegation to behold.

Serenia was not impressed by the wonders and lavish gifts sent her way. Many kings had the same treasures to brag about. Princess informed the Mulhorandi ambassador that only one thing could win her hand in marriage – if the king could present her the fabled unicorn of Ganathwood so the princess could ride on its back to the wedding. Sorenhotep consulted his royal magicians but was informed that no one could capture the beast; it was amazingly strong and fast, besting all hunters who had ever tried. Only a fair and pure maiden could distract the creature long enough for it to be harnessed. The Pharaoh ordered the kingdom's young maidens to be gathered and sent to Ganathwood, but months passed by, and none of the Mulhorandi girls were successful.

Princess Serenia was surprised to see the Mulhorandi diplomat return to her court with good news – the legendary unicorn was captured, but the princess had to travel to Ganathwood, where Sorenhotep kept the beast. Serenia complied and was greeted with no man, no soldier, just the forest. It did not take long for the fabled unicorn to present itself and lay its head on the princess's lap. Suddenly, the Mulhorandi soldiers leaped from their hiding and roped the unicorn.

Princess Serenia was outraged by the deception at first, but eventually came to a realization that the crafty Pharaoh did just what she asked of him and simply outwitted her. She agreed to the marriage and became Queen Serenia of Mulhorand.

By the, The Armoire of Queen Serenia of Mulhorand was one of the curios in the collection of magical items that belonged to Mintassan the Magnificent of Westgate. Serenia's humble armoire was displayed in his shop – Mintassan's Mysteries on the Market Triangle.

## Sentinel (axe)

Sentinel were a variety of enchanted battleaxes created on the Sword Coast North in the late 14 century DR.

Powers
There existed three variants of the Sentinel weapon, each possessed a +1, +2, and +8 enchantments what made the axe's blade deadlier and guided the wielder's hand towards the target. Each variety boosted the wielder's abilities to hear and see, and radiated a soft white glow within . Lastly, the two stronger versions allowed the wielder to cast the true seeing spell once and twice per day respectively.

Sentinel could be created from an enchanted battleaxe and a slab of adamantite.

History
Sentinel with a simpler +1 enchantment was forged by Master Blacksmith Marrok of Shining Knight Arms and Armor shop of Neverwinter to help the heroes who sought the cure from the dreaded Wailing Death of the . Later the same year, a more powerful version was created by Master Blacksmith Barun of Beorunna's Well to help the forces of Neverwinter stand against Lady Aribeth the Betrayer, who led a marching army of Luskan in the deadly but short-lived Neverwinter-Luskan war.

When the Neverwinter-Luskan war was over, the Hero of Neverwinter and their companions became famed across Neverwinter. One dwarven smith, whose name was not imprinted into the weapon, set out to create a powerful +8 Sentinel, to honor the heroes. As he was working on the axe, his body became consumed by fever that drove him to create enchantments of strength and complexity he could never achieve in the past. The smith claimed that his god possessed him and blessed the weapon. This powerful Sentinel was locked in a display at the Halls of Justice in Neverwinter, until, one day, it disappeared never to be seen again.

## Selgaunt/Foreign District

The Foreign District was a ward of Selgaunt dedicated to commerce and travelers, visitors, and foreigners in general.

Location
This district was located close to the center of Selgaunt, north of Temple Avenue.

Description
Like other sections of Selgaunt, the Foreign District was home to wood and stone or brick buildings that often had some kind of architectural flourish or another, as buildings in the city were typically designed to dazzle spectators. The building types of the district were predominated by boarding houses, inns, eateries, taverns, and equipment shops. Many shop fronts had painted signs hung outside, designed to attract the attention of newcomers. Some sported roadside animal pens.

Its borders with the other districts were denoted by an ancient stone wall of granite. By the 1370s DR this wall had deteriorated away to a mere thigh-height, making its separation more symbolic than literal, reflective of the Selgauntan contempt for foreigners.

Both its cobblestoned main thoroughfares and alleyways were wide, with the former able to accommodate the larger oxen drawn wagons that ferried goods to the wharves, farmers' wagons, and horse-drawn carts. Closer to Temple Avenue, the district's roads got narrower and its inns fewer. In some areas, the close by Hunting Gardens of the Hulorn dominated the skyline. Trees and streetlights dotted along the roads, illuminating the area at night.

In the late 14 century DR, Hulorn Andeth Ilchammar expressed his strange artistic tastes by having monstrous statuary placed in parts of the Foreign District. Examples of this included a bronze statue of a chimera on a raised pedestal and a stone fountain with a hydra centerpiece, whose waters were dyed and rigged to spurt out from the hydra's heads.

Law
The district was thoroughly patrolled by the Scepters of Selgaunt, so much so that the Zhentarim considered it to be a "hot zone".

Inhabitants
The Foreign District received foot traffic from all walks of life, including adventurers beggars, pickpockets and other rogues, laborers, merchants and traders, mercenaries, coinlasses, and even the occasional noble coach. Its permanent residents were most predominantly street vendors.

Notable Locations
Festhalls and Taverns
 Gilt Lizard, a quiet alehall and inn.
 Silver Lion, a lowrate taproom.
 The Workbench, a clapboard-sided tavern and eatery.
Streets & Alleys
 Colls Way
 Rauncel's Ride, one of the main thoroughfares of the district.
 Tormyn's Way, a road that led east from the Foreign District to the wide road of the Temple Avenue.
 Vesey Street
 Wide Way, another one of the district's main thoroughfares.

## Sea Reaver

The Sea Reaver was a variety of enchanted scythe that originated from the artifact once wielded by Igulik, a legendary Rashemaar hero long before the late 14 century DR.

Powers
There existed three variants of the Sea Reavers; each possessed a +1, +2, or +8 enchantment that made the scythe's blade deadlier and guided the wielder's hand towards the target. All three versions were ensorcelled to protect the wielder from elemental damage, but stronger variants had a deadly keen edge, and dealt additional and deadly sonic damage to elementals.

Creation
A Sea Reaver could be created from an enchanted scythe and a slab of adamantite.

History
The first Sea Reaver was a legendary weapon from Rashemen. The mighty hero Igulik received the weapon from a great water spirit of Lake Ashane to use it against an evil wizard that sought to enslave the tribes through the power of summoned elementals. As was traditional for Rashemaar legends, Igulik was successful in saving his people but died heroically after ending the wizard's life. When Igulik died, the Sea Reaver returned to the great spirit of Lake Ashane to be one day given to another hero.

More common versions of Sea Reavers with a simpler +1 enchantment were forged by Master Blacksmith Marrok of the Shining Knight Arms and Armor shop of Neverwinter to help the heroes who sought the cure for the dreaded Wailing Death of the . Later the same year, more powerful versions were created by Master Blacksmith Barun of Beorunna's Well to help the forces of Neverwinter stand against Lady Aribeth the Betrayer, who led a marching army of Luskan in the deadly but short-lived Neverwinter–Luskan war.

## Seaweed

Seaweed referred to a diverse array of marine algae found across the many seas of Toril,Despite appearances, seaweed in actuality is not really a type of plant. However, it has been misclassified as such across the editions of Dungeons & Dragons, including in many Realms sources, and thus is treated as such on this page. This miss-identification stems in part from seaweed, much like land plants, containing chlorophyll and relying on photosynthesis to survive. However, unlike plants they lack any "true" stems, roots, leaves, or vascular tissue. other worlds, and even other planes of existence.

Description
Most seaweed could be found growing in far spanning beds known as "forests", in seas as far down as, at depth zones where sunlight could be readily received. Some seaweed could also be found at the first few hundred feet beyond that, though it was limited to only the most hardiest of breeds.

On average seaweed grew to heights of . Their height and clustered density could cause minimal vision obstruction for creatures underwater. The average coloration of seaweed was varied brown and green hues, though they could also come in countless brilliant colors, such as purple.

Some, though not all, species of seaweed were edible and being able to identify which were which was considered an essential part of survival skills for any sailor. Other species could be quite poisonous if consumed.

Seaweed often found its way entangled in fishing nets, left to be discarded in harbors where it could be hazardous to ships and eventually lead to a smell of rotting seaweed lingering in the air. Some cities might have a group of people tasked with removing it, such as the Guild of Watermen who maintained Deepwater Harbor.

Species
Arame A species of seaweed that could be found in the waters of the Dragon Reach.
Armistice Seaweeds The planet Armistice in Winterspace was home to several species of seaweed that were highly toxic to non-native lifeforms.
Dleem An olive-green, stringy seaweed in the Sea of Swords that grew in starfish-shaped clumps. They partly clung to rocks and partly floated "adrift" in passing water. When roughly boiled a long period, the seaweed lost its tough, chewy consistency and would begin to take on the flavor of whatever it was boiled with.
Dulce An edible species of seaweed found in the Sea of Swords.
Eel Grass An edible species of seaweed found in the Sea of Swords.
Golden Angel Seaweed A species of seaweed that nereids were often mistaken for whilst submerged in water.
Hijiki A species of seaweed that could be found in the waters of the Dragon Reach.
Kelp A common edible subtype of seaweed for which there were a great variety of in the Realms, including magical species.
Kelpie A malevolently intelligent and carnivorous breed of seaweed that could shapeshift its form into a beautiful woman.While some sources may refer to kelpies as merely "resembling" seaweed, far more refer to them as outright being a kind of seaweed, such as the cited page of Tangled Webs, wherein the sailor Rethnor states, "This is a kelpie, a rare form of seaweed."
Kombu A species of seaweed that could be found in the waters of the Dragon Reach.
Nori An edible species of seaweed that could be found in the Celestial Sea.
Salt Ivy A species of seaweed unique to the Moonsea. Growing in clumps of green strands that measured  in length, it was considered a navigational hazard to ships and often fouled fishing nets.
Sargasso Seaweed Seaweed that drifted free-floatingly on the surface of the ocean, often clumping together in a large dense mass. In some cases they were so dense that one could walk upon them. They were often seen as a hazard to ships, slowing their movement or in the worst cases entrapping them, though this was mainly a concern for ships propelled by oars. Additionally, floating seaweed often attracted monstrous scavengers, such as lacedons, giant crabs, marine decapus, and the like.
Strangle weed A carnivorous breed of kelp with an animal-level of intellect that was found in relatively warm sea water. It was indistinguishable from normal seaweed.
Seaweed of Water Breathing Magical seaweed that granted the ability to temporarily breath underwater to those who ate it.
Related
Kelp angler A sentient, carnivorous breed of plant creatures that looked like jellyfish-shaped clusters of kelp.
Sluk A species of seaweed endemic to Wildspace. Rather than photosynthesis, they thrived off of magic. And much like sargasso seaweed was to terrestrial ships, it posed a hazard to the spelljammers that sailed through Wildspace.

Habitats
 In interior Faerûn, seaweed could be found across the temperate and tropical depths of the Sea of Fallen Stars, including in the areas known as the Dragon Reach and the Dragonmere.
 In north Faerûn, seaweed could be found in the Moonsea region's lake of the same name.
 In northwest Faerûn, seaweed could be found in Icewind Dale's Lac Dinneshere.
 In south Faerûn, seaweed could be found in great abundance in the Lake of Steam, wherein it grew in larger sizes and caused those creatures that fed upon it to grow larger in turn.
 In southwest Faerûn, seaweed could be found in the Shining Sea.
 In west Faerun, seaweed could be found in the Sea of Swords and the wider Trackless Sea that separated the continent from Maztica. And in the Sunset Mountains, Wolover's Keep had a lake surrounding it with a long stretch of floating seaweed, used to conceal the presence of strangle weed.
 In Thingulph and the waterways of the Thindol Basin.
 Beyond Faerûn, seaweed could be found in the seas of Kara-Tur and Zakhara, being most often encountered in coastal waters.
 On the Malatran Plateau in Kara-Tur, seaweed could be found in the Sleepy Lake.
 Elsewhere in Realmspace, the planet Karpri had a massive bed of floating seaweed and kelp in its equatorial region, so thick that it could support weights exceeding a few thousand pounds.
Beyond the Prime Material
 On Arborea, seaweed could be found in the oceanic layer Ossa.
 In the Domains of Dread, seaweed could be found within maritime domains. Most notably, Draga Salt-Biter's domain of Sargoss, whose waters were crowded by a gargantuan mass of sargasso seaweed, whose seabed was largely enshrouded by seaweed, and was home to a number of kelpies.
 On Mount Celestia, seaweed could be found in the oceanic layer Lunia.
 Among the Inner Planes, seaweed could be found in the Elemental Plane of Water, where it sometimes took the form of large floating spheres that grew equally in all directions.
 In the Outlands, seaweed could be found in Tir fo Thuinn, the divine realm of the Celtic god Manannan Mac Lir.
 On the plane of Elysium it could be found in the layer of Thalasia, where Surminare's divine realm of Selkies' Grotto was hidden beneath vast mazes of seaweed and kelp, and in the River Oceanus, where it flowed out of Thalasia and into the layer of Belierin.

Availability
Places in the Realms where one could purchase seaweed included:
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold dry seaweed under its "Wizards' Laboratory" section for 2 silver pieces per .
 In the markets of Immersea, taken from the Mistfisher Bay of the Dragonmere.
 In Caer-Dineval, where residents harvested edible seaweeds from Lac Dinneshere.
 In Daggerford, one could find seaweed for sale at the Flying Fish fish market.
 In Kara-Tur, on the island nations of Kozakura and Wa.
 On the island of Nimbral.
 On the island of Ruathym, where residents kept steady supplies of edible seaweed.
 In Ravens Bluff, though only members of the Fishmongers Association were allowed to publicly sell or barter seaweed as it was classified as a type of seafood.
 In Waterdeep, Felhaur's Fine Fish sold jars of pickled seaweed.

Uses
Camouflage

 Some creatures had natural body colorations that lent themselves well to blending in amongst seaweed, such as giant pikes, the spines of pungi rays, and weed eels. The latter, when gathered in large groups, could even be mistaken for tangled masses of seaweed.
 Through use of their color shifting abilities, giant octopi often disguised their tentacles as seaweed.
 Some ambush predators, such as giant pikes, giant marine spiders, and will o'seas would hide within beds of seaweed waiting for their prey.
 The most preferred area of combat amongst aquatic elves was forests of seaweed, for their typically greenish-silver skin and often stringy hair, which ranged in complementary shades of green and blue, helped them to blend in and hide amongst the weeds just as well as surface elves hid amongst the trees. They also had a propensity for hiding all manner of spring-loaded booby-traps and magical entrapments within beds of seaweed.
 Some aquatic creatures made their homes near thick seaweed beds due to the concealment they afforded, such as locathah, hai nu, and reavers.

Clothing
 Seaweed was commonly woven into clothing by nixies, sirines, some aquatic elves, and some marids.
 In the case of nixies, they typically wove colorful seaweed into tight-fitting wraps for women and loincloths for men.
 Ningyo used lengths of woven seaweed in the bracelets and necklaces they fashioned.
 In the undersea realms of Serôs, all aquatic races commonly wore accessories fashioned from woven kelp fronds, including jewelry, belts, harnesses, and bandoliers.
 Some Umberlants wore clothing woven out of seaweed, carried accessories wrapped in it, or simply had decorative seaweed designs on their clothes.
Food
 Seaweed was widely consumed by many animals and humanoids as either a primary or supplementary part of their diets. These included giant octopi, giant otters, hai nu, hippocampi, island giants, lung wangs, ningyo, pahari, sea sprites, selkies, and zoveri.
 Across the Sword Coast the species dleem, due to its ability to take on the flavor of whatever it was boiled with, was often used to bulk up soups, stews, and other dishes. In Waterdeep in particular, it was used as part of Tharval-and-dleem.
 Kelp in particular was a staple in the diets of many of the aquatic races of Serôs, going as far back as the early days of the Aryselmalyr Empire, where the aquatic elven rulers taught many how to cultivate and grow it, even in places that it was previously never found naturally. It was actively farmed by merfolk, shalarin, and the Noble Malenti of Kymal. Beyond Serôs, seaweed was also actively farmed in the seas surrounding Zakhara by the pahari.
 The Blushing Mermaid in Baldur's Gate served a rather revolting small-fish stew consisting of rotting baitfish boiled with sea salt and seaweed.
 Braethyn, a thick broth in Northkeep that used sea ivy.
 Seaweed was used by the alesmith Bowser to create the exotic brew Seaweed Ale.
 Among the islands of the Crowded Sea, those who worshiped the savage god Pag restricted themselves to a diet of fish and seaweed.
 In parts of Kara-Tur, seaweed was collected by fisherfolk to be consumed raw or dried for use in many ways. Kozakurans used sheets of dried nori, for example, by crumbling them into their rice or using them as a wrapper into which they stuffed rice. Wanese also used dried seaweed to make flour. The Zantira on the Malatran Plateau used it in some dishes. And Kozakurans used seaweed in some forms of sushi, which saw popularity outside its native country in the Hai Yuan Province of Shou Lung and Flirin's Sea Morsels in Ravens Bluff.
 The Moonstone Mask offered patrons fried dulce seaweed.
 Nimbrians harvested various types of seaweed for use in teas and wines.
 Seaweed salad, a type of salad that heavily featured seaweed.
 Seaweed could be used to produce sauces. In the village of Uthmere, residents produced strongly flavored sauces from concoctions of herbs, spices, Great Dale wildflowers, and diced and cooked seaweed. And in Ravens Bluff, Flirin of Flirin's Sea Morsels made seaweed sauces using secret recipes that she herself had devised.
 In the Sembian village of Tulbegh, farmers were renowned for the baby food that they produced by mixing osquip milk from a mulch of seaweed harvested from the Sea of Fallen Stars.

Housing
 The aquatic elves who lived near Evermeet constructed some of their buildings out of seaweed.
 Nixies, beyond just weaving seaweed into clothing, typically used the material to construct their homes.
 Ningyo sometimes constructed temporary shelters by lining underwater ledges with seaweed.
 When not residing in natural caverns, the nomadic locathah of Serôs commonly wove tents out of hydrozoan branches and kelp fronds.
 The shalarins of Serôs coated kelp fronds in a pearl-like substance, hardening it into a tough material, which they would then apply as siding to their architecture in a style reminescent of what Surface Worlders called "wattle and daub".
 Beds of floating seaweed sometimes served as a lair for aquatic creatures, such as sea hags. Likewise, the balls of seaweed on the Plane of Water were sometimes used by native outsiders, such as tritons, as lairs.

Magic
 Seaweed was so commonly of use to wizards that Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold dry seaweed under its "Wizards' Laboratory".
 Certain seaweeds were quite valuable to alchemists.
 Seaweed could be affected by most spells that manipulated vegetation, such as charm plants, entangle and recover trail.
 Arame, hijiki, and kombu seaweed could all be used as material components in certain spells.It is difficult to ascertain exactly what spells these "spell components" being carried by the Istishian druids Bulclutha and Malstrua would be used for. Most of their listed spells required only verbal and somatic components, whilst those that didn't like create water or stone shape called for materials that weren't vegetation. The one spell that did call for a plant that could feasibly be substituted with seaweed was slow poison, which called for a bud of garlic. Likewise, salt ivy could be used as a component in certain water-based spells.
 Clutter path could substitute seaweed in place of the weeds required to cast it.
 Sargasso, a spell that could conjure a thick bed of sargasso seaweed, required a small amount of seaweed as a material component.
 Samprey's sensible sea sphere required a necklace fashioned from kelp as a spell component.
 Sarphoon's seaspell could transform seaweed and seawater into exact duplicates of nearby living fish.
 Under candle magic, seaweed could serve as one of the three ingredients needed to produce a candle with the effect of divination.
 A variation of Keoghtom's ointment could be created using seaweed.
 Sea hags could create simulacrums of other humanoids using a variety of flotsam, such as seaweed, slime, and half-eaten fish.
 Serôsian wizards used mixtures of magical kelp to create the inks they wrote spell scrolls with.
 Aquatic elf wizards frequently used papyrus-like sheets of thick, fibrous seaweed for their spell scrolls. These were alchemically treated so as to prevent rotting and water damage.
 To get around the hurdles of imbibing in a fluid volume, both Serôsian wizards and aquatic elf wizards in other seas produced unguents, such as thick gelatinous pastes of kelp, to mimic potions. Additionally, the aquatic elves of Faerûn bred magical seaweed that could mimic the effects of potions when ingested.
 The magical tome Lions of Tomorrow had parchment consisting of seaweed soaked in a solution of iodine in order to ward off Geomantic divinations.

Medicine
 Some seaweed could be used to produce healing salves.
 Some seawoods could be used to produce cures against poisons.
 Kelp in particular could be used treat thyroid glands and goiter, as well as applied internally or externally to relieve rheumatism.
Miscellaneous
 The aquatic elves of Aumrauth constructed what they called "seawater sleeves" out of weaving whale rib-cages with seaweed, using them to transport "deep-caught" fish from the waters of the Shining Sea up to the surface for trade.
 Bales of kelp were commonly used as a trading good in the barter economies of Serôs.
 Imroad Gaskulyn of Saerloon used various seaweeds alongside gravel, wines, and vinegars to concoct pastes that imparted a patina, tarnish, or wear to the small metal items he produced forgeries of.
 Merfolk sometimes used kelp and other seaweed in the construction of furniture, such as using it for padding in chairs and reclining sofas or filling clam-shaped "beds" filled with pallets of the stuff.
 Both crabfolk and yurians were known to weave stuff out of seaweed.
 Some kuo-toa fashioned fossilised shells into makeshift shields by wrapping a sticky, rope-like seaweed around them.
 Some merfolk wove tapestries out of seaweed.
 In the Ruined Kingdoms region of Zakhara, prior to the 14 century DR, it was not an uncommon practice to use seaweed to make parchment.
 Certain seaweeds were quite valued by scent-makers, who used them in the production of sachets.
 Some sahuagins enchanted seaweed so that it would entangle and trap those who wandered close to their lairs.
 Seaweed could be knotted together into nets, ropes, and rope ladders.
 Whenever it was time for Umberlants to remove offerings from the stone altars found in shrines of Umberlee, they would sluice buckets full of sea water and seaweed to signify that the Sea Queen had taken what was rightfully hers.

Trivia
 Duthlah'hass, a type of osssra, had a smell reminescent of burning seaweed.
 The Maztican goddess Watil's portfolio of plant-life included seaweed.
 Marels, merrow, saltwater trolls, sartani, sea hags, sea sprites, and water gen all exhibited hair that bore a resemblance to seaweed. In some cases, even the hair of a sea elf could be mistaken for seaweed.
 Sea druids, the specialty priests of Deep Sashelas, were skilled in identifying aquatic plants like seaweed and in effortlessly swimming through beds of them without leaving any sort of trail.
 The venom of a lock lurker had a smell reminiscent of seaweed.

## Sergol

Sergol was a priest of Tyr who served under Reverend Judge Oleff Uskar of the Hall of Justice in the late 14 century DR. Despite him being a priest, Sergol's magic was not of a healing nature.

Description
Sergol was an aged human man of musular physicue and a light skin tone. He had a bushy blonde beard and a full head of blonde hair. He wore a typical purple chainmails of the Tyrran faith of Neverwinter.

History
In the, Sergol was instructed by the Lady Hero of Neverwinter, Aribeth de Tylmarande, to assist the person who would become the Hero of Neverwinter during their quest to stop the Wailing plague that was consuming the city. Sergol's divine magics were empowerinf the Stone of Recall that would allow the Hero and their companions to be teleported back to the Hall of Justice and back at will during the quest. As the true cause of the Wailing Death were revealed, Sergol accompanied Lady Aribeth and the Hero across the Sword Coast North and aided them during the Luskan-Neverwinter war that erupted shortly after.

## Seamus

Seamus was a hermit who dwelled in the Lost Souls' Swamp, near the King's Forest of Cormyr.

Description
After living for decades in the swamp, Seamus was a somewhat ragged elder man.

Personality

Seamus was quite grumpy and preferred his solitude over strangers barging into "his" swamp. He was more than a little selfish, though this sense of self-preservation had kept him alive in dangerous conditions.

History

By the mid-1480s DR, Seamus had become a hermit and lived in Lost Souls' Swamp for over twenty years, learning everything there was to know about surviving there. During this time he became an associate of Oriphaun Huntsilver, helping him find lost relics in the past with his hermit-derived wisdom. This trust led Oriphaun to leave a piece of the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll with Seamus, counting on his secretive nature and the desolate swamp itself to keep it safe. While Seamus did as requested and hid it near the Fallen Oak Shrine, the magical power of the Scroll attracted the fell medusa Sshaastess, who took it and started exerting control over the swamp and woods nearby.

When Eradin Krall sent a team to investigate, they found Seamus and got directions to the medusa, meeting him again at the fallen tree that served as the entrance. Seamus pointedly refused to get involved in any combat with magically-enhanced medusa warlords, and waited nearby until the Harpers emerged victorious. He then offered to "clean up" her cave full of treasure.

## Seiveril's Crusade

Seiveril's Crusade was an elven military expedition organized by Lord Seiveril Miritar of Elion, that was formed in Evermeet and fought at Evereska, Myth Glaurach and Myth Drannor. After fighting against the forces of evil across the breadth of Faerûn, including the sprawling Cormanthor War, it ultimately achieved the reclamation of Myth Drannor.

Relationships
In the final march on Myth Drannor, the Crusade worked with the Elves of Cormanthor, an alliance of local elves that in turn aligned with the drow of Eilistraee's Wanderers. These elves were not nearly as martially-minded as the Crusade, instead being mainly focused on the exploration and reconstruction of the ruins.

History
The Crusade was born on Ches 10 of 1374 DR when Seiveril Miritar resigned from the royal council of Evermeet, forming a volunteer army to relieve Evereska from the assaults of House Dlardrageth. The elves began arriving in Evereska as soon as Ches 16, joining in battle and driving the fey'ri off by Tarsakh 12. They then moved to assault the ruins of Myth Glaurach, where the orc and ogre allies of House Dlardrageth had been based, arriving by Tarsakh 19.

On Mirtul 19, Araevin Teshurr discovered the hidden portal network that Sarya Dlardrageth had used to move between Myth Drannor and Glaurachyndaar, and on Mirtul 24 Seiveril called a Council of War with his commanders that set the course of the Crusade. They used a portal to take the Crusade to Semberholme, and on Mirtul 28 Seiveril reaffirmed the Dales Compact with Battledale, Deepingdale, Mistledale, and Shadowdale, and then set marching to Myth Drannor on Kythorn 1. In response to this pressing threat, House Dlardrageth formed alliances with Hillsfar and Sembia that led to them joining the war by Kythorn 4, and on Kythorn 12 Hillsfar in turn allied with Zhentil Keep to bring them in as well.

This grand alliance against the Elven Crusade had shattered by Kythorn 21, as Hillsfar had only joined with the intention of luring Zhentil Keep into a losing war. The plan worked—on Kythorn 24 the Crusade defeated Scyllua Darkhope's army, the withdrawal of Hillsfar's forces sent the greater campaign into disarray, and Zhentil Keep began warring with Hillsfar instead. These conflicts drew attention in nearby lands, with House Sashenstar taking bids from every side of the wars for logistics and supplies.

By Eleasis 5 the conflict Sembia formed a truce with the Crusade, which turned into a new alliance against Dlardrageth by Eleasis 11. With the odds now reversed, the Crusade managed to assault the Vale of Lost Voices on day 17, reached Myth Drannor on day 20, and took the city on day 22 of that month. Though almost all of House Dlardrageth was slain, Seiveril Miritar also fell in battle.

Now called the Army of Myth Drannor, the far-from-home elves were put on a defensive stance from Eleint 3 as Zhentil Keep finished its brief war with Hillsfar and sought to prevent the elves from securing their new foothold. Facing not just the Zhents but their new drow Masked Brigades from House Jaelre and Clan Auzkovyn, both sides were bled by constant raids and strikes. Despite the arrival of the Knights of Myth Drannor and the crowning of Ilsevele Miritar as the new Coronal, the situation remained poor as the Cormanthor War dragged on, only ending in 1376 DR when Zhentil Keep and the drow cut their losses and accepted peace terms.

A quality four-story building, it was located on the edge of Farix Drudach, Otahl Sabban, in the city's Emerald Ward.

## Seven Gulders Caravan Coster

The Seven Gulders Caravan Coster was a business based in Yallasch in the Border Kingdoms in the late 14 century DR.

Though there were no outward signs, the coster was directly controlled by the leadership of the Rundeen, a mercantile organization, known as the Yrshelem. The only clue was that it was named for the gulder, a Tethyrian gold piece, indicating it had once been an independent business that had been taken over and renamed.

## Seven Burnings

The Seven Burnings was a short war of rebellion followed by devastating reprisals in the Shoon Imperium colonies of the Shaar region in the 5 century DR.

Location
The conflict encompassed the port city of Sheirtalar, and the interior towns of Sheirlantar, Kormul, Lhesper, Sebben, Rethmar, and Channathgate along the River Shaar and Lake Lhespen that rose in rebellion against the Shoon.

History
The Lapaliiya region came under Shoon control in 285 DR, but became near-independent in the lax reign of Qysara Shaani (367–427 DR).

As Qysar Amahl Shoon VII restored order in the early 5 century, the subjugated peoples of the Shaar colonies rose up against the oppressors. In the  they managed to kill the officials and soldiers that oversaw the settlements, ending Shoon rule in the Shaar. Qysar Amahl responded in the latter half of the  by sending his ruthless Qayadin Hakam yn Sarak el Sallah to restore order with a fleet of seventeen troop ships, and in the course of only two months he restored the dominion of the Imperium. First landing at Sheirtalar, his soldiers slew every rebel and much of the rest of the population, razing the city in the process. He then moved up the Shaar River and repeated the slaughter at Kormul, leading to the surrender of the remaining five rebel towns. Seeking to enact a lesson in power, the Bloody Qayadin continued to assault and destroy the remaining five settlements despite the end of hostilities. During this campaign, his Fanged Shield of Shyk Korort became a symbol of terror and tyranny in the region as it was used against the locals.

With the rebellion (and everyone else) thoroughly quashed, Hakam settled there as the nazir, or prefect, governing the region thence. Shoon soldiers were largely withdrawn from the region afterwards. Hakam lasted until the, when news of the fall of the Imperium reached the colonies. He was immediately torn apart by the widows of those he had slain, using naught but their bare hands.

The campaign was commemorated in the Sadimmin and Nallojal of Calimshan, with the Torch of the Seven Burnings campaign decoration for veteran soldiers and sailors.

In later ages, it was speculated that the undead of the Lhespenbog were derived from the rebels who were cornered and slaughtered there during the Burnings.

## Serena Worthwood

Serena Worthwood was an adventurer from Ravens Bluff in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Petite and slender, she didn't look her strength. Serena had dark brown hair and green eyes.

Personality
Serena was curious and obstinate.

Abilities
She was a warrior and a sneak both, with great agility for a thief, and strong enough to gain an advantage in combat. She had specialized in two-weapon fighting.

Possessions
The half-elf bore a cloak of elvenkind and carried a potion of flying. Notably, she had a heirloom silvered long sword.

Activities
By the late 14 century DR, she had become an adventurer in Ravens Bluff.

Relationships
Serena's father was an adventurer.

She was quite comforted by Aliana Meriadeth's quiet wisdom, and had in turn become the only person to learn she'd been a werefox. She thought Akira Iko was not happy amongst them, probably because of something that happened prior. Crakadan Earthdigger reminded her of her dad, and she liked him. She didn't know if Taldar Everwind's feelings for her were serious, and didn't want to return them if they weren't; he'd also taught her thief skills. She found Tanis Obereck's principles interesting, if only in context of his childhood.

History
Serena's human father had met his mother while rescuing her and other elves from evil giants; he worked as a constable throughout her childhood. Both her parents taught her the good of all was more important than how it was achieved.

She met Tanis Obereck at the age of sixteen, and later began adventuring together with him and Taldar Everwind. Three years later, her family moved to Sembia, and she stayed behind to keep adventuring. Financial difficulties had recently required her to sell off her thieves' tools.

Appendix
Background
Serena Worthwood originally appeared in Polyhedron #113, with only one minor difference: her native city, which was from a home setting in use by Robert Wiese rather than the Forgotten Realms. These characters were adapted for the Adventurer's Guild version of the Special Delivery module in 1998, and were reused for at least one more module, Legacy of Venom, in 1999.

## Sensitivity to psychic impressions

Sensitivity to psychic impressions was a psionic power of the clairsentience discipline that allowed a manifester to detect and peer into psychic residue that's been impressed upon a location by potent and highly dramatic events.

Effects
With this power, a manifester could perceive the "psychic residue" lingering in an area from powerful emotions that were experienced there, granting them momentary visions of the person who experienced that emotion. Past everyday occurrences could not be detected and seen by a manifester, as they didn't leave behind any psychic residue. Only significant and highly dramatic events, such as deaths, battles, betrayals, childbirth, marriages, murders, other moments of great pain, or just about any other event where one emotion dominates. The vision of the past will be vague and shadowy to the manifester, as if viewing a dream, not offering any special knowledge of those involved. But the dominant emotion involved will be recognized very clearly.

Beginning with the most recent event and working backward, the manifester could sense distinct events from several centuries back, with this range extending at a minimum 100 years. One distinct event could be sensed and viewed for every 10 minutes that the manifester spent maintaining concentration.

For older versions of sensitivity to psychic impressions, a manifestation completely backfiring would result in a ghost appearing. The angry spirit would attempt to magic jar the psionicist.

Displays
This power manifested standard auditory and material displays.

Users
This power was primarily knowable by psions who specialized as seers and spiritualists, though it was also found to be exhibited by some wild talents.

This power was exhibited as an innate psionic ability by greater shedu.

Known/Notable Users
 Anthraxus, a powerful altraloth.
 Charon, the boatman of the River Styx.
 Talib the Lich-Killer, a wily old Zakharan wild talent and necromancer of the deathslayer variety.
 Tithonnas, a legendary great wyrm topaz dragon.

Appendix
See Also
 Object reading, a clairsentient power that allowed a manifester to view psychic impressions from inanimate objects.

## Sea Reavers

The Sea Reavers were a long-running succession of pirate fleets operating out of the Nelanther Isles in the Sea of Swords of west Faerûn as of the late 14 century DR. Circa 1370 DR, they were a subsidiary of the Rundeen mercantile consortium.

History
Early Versions
Pirate fleets of the Nelanther Isles known as the Sea Reavers have existed since the early 1 century DR. The earliest to hold the name was reported to have participated in the attack on Ithalyr led by the second Black Alaric that led to the fall of House Ithal in the . They would continue, in varying forms, down through the centuries.

Their longest-lasting incarnation to date was active in the 14 century DR. At one point, they were led by Captain Kesmer Red-Eyed, called the "Coast Wolf". That was until the fateful day he docked at the pirate isle of Skaug and took aboard the adventurer Mintiper Moonsilver. Soon after, a storm drove Kesmer's ship onto rocks, wrecking it. Only one pirate survived, having been hurled well clear of the rocks by the impact. Thus, he was witness to what came next: a great magical flame burst up around Mintiper, then engulfed Kesmer and the whole crew. When the flames faded, all were consumed by the fire and Mintiper had vanished. The pirates believed Mintiper had killed the Coast Wolf and the rest by means of trickery and magical fire. (Mintiper himself escaped, telling acquaintances that an enchanted green gem gifted him by his mother had activated to save him, consuming the pirates and itself in order to teleport him away to the Purple Hills headlands.) After that, many thought the Sea Reavers had been obliterated for good.

Modern History
Yet they would soon find a new captain. Amidst the turbulent Tethyrian Interregnum after 1347 DR, a commander of Tethyr's royal navy named Toramir Kuurgan felt betrayed by the nobility for their selfishness and therefore betrayed his fallen kingdom. Circa 1350 DR, Toramir reassembled his flotilla with four ships and 200 crewmembers and officers loyal to him, and permitted safe passage back to the Dragon's Neck peninsula for any who refused and remained allegiant to the navy—only four departed. The new Sea Reavers joined with the survivors of the old, and operated as pirates for the next two decades. In time, their fleet grew to five vessels.

The Sea Reavers were amongst those Nelantheran pirate groups who held sway over the leaders of Tulmene, a small shipbuilding town on the Dragon's Neck. For some twenty years, they made the place a secret safe harbor for their kind. But in Eleasias of the, the newly acclaimed warrior-queen of Tethyr, Zaranda Star, arrived in Tulmene on her tour of the realm and soon discovered the truth. She and her band rallied the townsfolk and battled the pirates at the harbor, finally driving them out to sea. Two of the Sea Reavers' ships were damaged in the fighting, and when they were forced to enter the Race and its intense winds and currents, they were sunk.

Incensed by the loss of ships and crewmen and corrupted by two decades of crime, Toramir had no desire to rejoin in the reformation of Tethyr and instead swore vengeance against the new queen, even to see her dead. While some pirates like Captain Bahija the Fair shared this view, most others saw peace in Tethyr as good for business and therefore good for them. This put the Sea Reavers at odds with the other Nelantheran pirate factions. In any case, soon after the expulsion, the daughter of the newly appointed mayor of Tulmene, Jos Knightson, was kidnapped at sea. Those responsible in the Shadow Thieves forced Jos to begin reestablishing links with the pirates including the Sea Reavers.

Nevertheless, the Sea Reavers encountered difficulties supplying and maintaining their remaining ships and so in the late 1360s DR Toramir made a deal to work for the Rundeen mercantile consortium.

But with Tethyr's restoration in the, and a rebuilt strong and capable navy, pirates like the Sea Reavers found its shipping too hard a target and so turned to prey upon that of Amn. In late Eleint, the Sea Reavers attacked and looted four ships of a seven-strong fleet belonging to the Vemmil family of Amn, which were returning from a voyage to Zakhara. The other three made it to Murann. Some time the following year, under the auspices of the Rundeen, Toramir and Bahija made alliances with the ogre mage Sythillis of the Sythillisian Empire to blockade Amnian ports like Murann to prevent treasure ships fleeing, in exchange for a cut of that treasure, as well as stop the Amnians retaking the city. They hoped to acquire ships and a new safe port on the Sword Coast. By 1372 DR, the Rundeen and their pet Nelanther pirates were using Murann as a safe haven and defending it against naval counter-attack.

Organization
In the mid-to-late 14 century DR, the then-current incarnation of the Sea Reavers were commanded by Captain Toramir Kuurgan. A formerly lawful Tethyrian naval commander, he grew to hate his former kingdom and was driven to madness and paranoia.

Activities
Although once quite varied in their tactics and actions, they were far less so by 1370 DR under Toramir. By the terms of their agreement with the Rundeen, in each summer trade season they were required to dispatch at least one pirate ship to the Shining Sea to prey on non-Rundeen merchant shipping.

Base of Operations
They were primarily based on the island of Ioma in the Nelanthers. Whilst affiliated with the Rundeen, they could get their ships refitted in Rundeen-controlled ports in or near the Border Kingdoms.

Possessions
Circa 1370 DR, the Sea Reavers operated five ships.

## Seed Ceremony

The Seed Ceremony was a ritual performed by followers of Chauntea to wed themselves, completed by planting a seed together and reciting the proper vows.

Description
As long as one had belief in Chauntea and the barest of materials required to sow a seed, this ceremony could be performed anywhere. There was an invocation recited in turns by the two to be married while holding the seed between their palms and then planting and watering the seed together. The recitation was as follows:

A kiss may then be shared between the newly-weds.

History
In the, Tanalasta Obarskyr and Rowen Cormaeril performed the ceremony in secret in order to unite themselves against the wishes of the royal family. During their travels, they prepared a small patch of dirt by hand, using water from their waterskin, and a single columbine seed to perform the ceremony. Even though there were no witnesses or clergy present, the ceremony was considered for all intents and purposes to be a legitimate political union.
